- name: Erythrocyte GLUT1 Dysfunction
  description: >-
    Erythrocytes express the same GLUT1 transporter as the brain endothelium, so
    the haploinsufficient state is measurable outside the brain as reduced
    3-O-methyl-D-glucose uptake (typically about half of control Vmax with an
    unchanged Km) and, with flow cytometry, as reduced surface GLUT1. Residual
    uptake tracks clinical severity. Certain pore-region alleles additionally
    render the mutant transporter leaky to cations, disturbing red-cell sodium,
    potassium and calcium and producing echinocytosis and hemolysis. This node
    is chiefly a diagnostic surrogate of the primary lesion; only the cation
    leak branch is itself pathogenic.
  role: mediator
  biological_scale: CELLULAR
  mechanism_confidence: ESTABLISHED
  cell_types:
  - preferred_term: Erythrocyte
    term:
      id: CL:0000232
      label: erythrocyte
  biological_processes:
  - preferred_term: D-glucose transmembrane transport
    term:
      id: GO:1904659
      label: D-glucose transmembrane transport
    modifier: DECREASED
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:15622525
    reference_title: "Glut-1 deficiency syndrome: clinical, genetic, and therapeutic aspects."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: IN_VITRO
    snippet: "The mean V(max) for the 3-O-methyl-D-glucose uptake into erythrocytes was 996 fmol/10(6) red blood cells per second, significantly less (54 +/- 11%; t test, p < 0.05) than the mean control value of 1,847. The mean Km value for the patient group (1.4 +/- 0.5mM) was similar to the control group (1.7 +/- 0.5mM; t test, p > 0.05)."
    explanation: Kinetic assay in 16 patients shows roughly half-normal transport capacity with unchanged affinity, the erythrocyte signature of haploinsufficiency.
  - reference: PMID:22190371
    reference_title: "Glut1 deficiency syndrome and erythrocyte glucose uptake assay."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "We also noted a significant correlation between the mean erythrocyte 3-OMG uptake and clinical severity (R(2) = 0.94)."
    explanation: In 109 suspected cases, residual erythrocyte uptake correlates tightly with clinical severity.
  - reference: PMID:18451999
    reference_title: "GLUT1 mutations are a cause of paroxysmal exertion-induced dyskinesias and induce hemolytic anemia by a cation leak."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: IN_VITRO
    snippet: "the hemolysis with echinocytosis may result from alterations in intracellular electrolytes caused by a cation leak through mutant GLUT1."
    explanation: Identifies the cation-leak branch by which a pore-region GLUT1 allele damages the erythrocyte itself.
  downstream:
  - target: Hemolytic anemia
    causal_link_type: DIRECT
    description: >-
      Restricted to alleles that create a cation leak (e.g. Q282_S285del);
      alleles that only reduce transport (A275T, G314S) do not cause hemolysis.
    hypothesis_groups:
    - erythrocyte_cation_leak_hemolysis
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:18451999
      reference_title: "GLUT1 mutations are a cause of paroxysmal exertion-induced dyskinesias and induce hemolytic anemia by a cation leak."
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
      snippet: "We screened 4 additional families, in which PED is combined with epilepsy, developmental delay, or migraine, but not with hemolysis or echinocytosis, and identified 2 additional GLUT1 mutations (A275T, G314S) that decreased glucose transport but did not affect cation permeability."
      explanation: Allele-level dissociation shows hemolysis follows the cation leak, not the transport loss per se.
- name: Arrested Cerebral Angiogenesis and Microvascular Diminution
  description: >-
    In Glut1-deficient mice, low endothelial GLUT1 arrests postnatal cerebral
    angiogenesis and leaves a profoundly reduced brain microvasculature while
    barrier integrity is preserved. Pre-symptomatic AAV9-mediated GLUT1
    repletion averts the vascular defect and the disease; late (adult) repletion
    does not. The 2020 consensus statement adopts impaired microvascular
    development as a complication of delayed treatment, but direct human
    vascular data are lacking, so this node is provisional and is the
    structural basis of an emerging, non-fuel mechanism.
  role: mediator
  biological_scale: TISSUE
  mechanism_confidence: PROVISIONAL
  locations:
  - preferred_term: brain
    term:
      id: UBERON:0000955
      label: brain
  cell_types:
  - preferred_term: Brain Microvascular Endothelial Cell
    term:
      id: CL:2000044
      label: brain microvascular endothelial cell
  biological_processes:
  - preferred_term: cerebral angiogenesis
    term:
      id: GO:0001525
      label: angiogenesis
    modifier: DECREASED
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:28106060
    reference_title: "Brain microvasculature defects and Glut1 deficiency syndrome averted by early repletion of the glucose transporter-1 protein."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    snippet: "Studies to define the temporal requirements for Glut1 reveal that pre-symptomatic, AAV9-mediated repletion of the protein averts brain microvasculature defects and prevents disease, whereas augmenting the protein late, during adulthood, is devoid of benefit."
    explanation: Temporal rescue experiments show the microvascular defect is GLUT1-dependent and has a developmental window.
  - reference: PMID:32913944
    reference_title: "Glut1 Deficiency Syndrome (Glut1DS): State of the art in 2020 and recommendations of the international Glut1DS study group."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    snippet: "Impaired development of the brain microvasculature is one such complication of delayed Glut1DS treatment in the postnatal period."
    explanation: The consensus statement carries the mouse finding into the clinical framing of untreated early disease; it is expert opinion, not human vascular measurement.
  downstream:
  - target: Impaired Neurodevelopment and Movement Disorder
    causal_link_type: INDIRECT_UNKNOWN_INTERMEDIATES
    description: >-
      A diminished microvascular bed is proposed to compound the fuel deficit
      and contribute to cognitive dysfunction; the human contribution is
      unquantified.
    hypothesis_groups:
    - microvascular_developmental_defect
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:28106060
      reference_title: "Brain microvasculature defects and Glut1 deficiency syndrome averted by early repletion of the glucose transporter-1 protein."
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
      snippet: "Glut1 repletion in early-symptomatic mutants that have experienced sustained periods of low brain glucose nevertheless restores the cerebral microvasculature and ameliorates disease."
      explanation: Restoring the microvasculature ameliorates disease in symptomatic mutants, linking the vascular node to the neurodevelopmental outcome.
- name: Exertion- and Fasting-Sensitive Basal Ganglia Energy Failure
  description: >-
    Under sustained exertion or fasting, the marginal glucose supply of the
    energy-deficient brain is transiently overwhelmed in corticostriatal
    circuits, producing episodes of choreoathetosis or dystonia affecting mainly
    the legs (paroxysmal exercise-induced dyskinesia). The same triggers
    aggravate seizures and paroxysmal ataxia. Functional imaging in PED
    families implicates altered glucose metabolism in the corticostriate
    pathways; the exertion-induced energy-deficit model is proposed rather than
    directly measured in the basal ganglia.
  role: mediator
  biological_scale: TISSUE
  mechanism_confidence: PROVISIONAL
  locations:
  - preferred_term: basal ganglia
    term:
      id: UBERON:0002420
      label: basal ganglion
  cell_types:
  - preferred_term: Neuron
    term:
      id: CL:0000540
      label: neuron
  biological_processes:
  - preferred_term: Generation of Precursor Metabolites and Energy
    term:
      id: GO:0006091
      label: generation of precursor metabolites and energy
    modifier: DECREASED
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:18577546
    reference_title: "Paroxysmal exercise-induced dyskinesia and epilepsy is due to mutations in SLC2A1, encoding the glucose transporter GLUT1."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Functional imaging studies implicated alterations in glucose metabolism in the corticostriate pathways in the pathophysiology of PED and in the frontal lobe cortex in the pathophysiology of epileptic seizures."
    explanation: Imaging in SLC2A1 PED families localises the metabolic disturbance behind dyskinesia to corticostriatal circuits.
  - reference: PMID:26193382
    reference_title: "From splitting GLUT1 deficiency syndromes to overlapping phenotypes."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Sensitivity to fasting or exertion in combination with those 3 main symptoms were the main differences between mutated and negative patients (p < 0.001)."
    explanation: In a national laboratory cohort, trigger sensitivity to fasting and exertion discriminates SLC2A1-positive from negative patients.
  - reference: PMID:23443458
    reference_title: "Phenotypic spectrum of glucose transporter type 1 deficiency syndrome (Glut1 DS)."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "A clue to the diagnosis in patients with paroxysmal symptoms may be the triggering of episodes during fasting or exercise."
    explanation: Review confirms fasting and exercise as the characteristic provocations of the paroxysmal phenotype.
  downstream:
  - target: Paroxysmal Exercise/Exertion-Induced Dyskinesia

      explanation: Describes the clinical form of the exertion-triggered episodes produced by this mechanism.

  description: >-

    responsive to ketogenic diet therapy.

    reference_title: "Glucose transporter-1 deficiency syndrome: the expanding clinical and genetic spectrum of a treatable disorder."
    supports: SUPPORT

    explanation: In 57 genetically confirmed patients, 84% had the epilepsy-containing classical phenotype and 15% had no epilepsy, keeping seizures in the VERY_FREQUENT band while documenting the epilepsy-free minority.

- category: Laboratory
  name: Hypoglycorrhachia (low CSF glucose)
  description: >-
    Low CSF glucose (typically below 2.5 mmol/L, mean about 1.7 mmol/L) with a
    CSF:blood glucose ratio usually below 0.5 in the presence of normal blood
    glucose. Present in essentially all genetically confirmed patients when
    age-specific reference values are applied, so it is the diagnostic
    laboratory phenotype; rare reports of apparently normal CSF glucose exist.
  phenotype_term:
    preferred_term: Hypoglycorrhachia
    term:
      id: HP:0011972
      label: Hypoglycorrhachia
  frequency: VERY_FREQUENT

    supports: SUPPORT

    explanation: All 57 genetically confirmed patients had hypoglycorrhachia, supporting VERY_FREQUENT.
  - reference: PMID:23999624
    reference_title: "Cerebrospinal fluid analysis in the workup of GLUT1 deficiency syndrome: a systematic review."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "The CSF glucose levels ranged from 16.2 to 50.5 mg/dL and were at or below the 10th percentile for all 147 patients."
    explanation: Systematic review of 147 genetically proven patients confirms universally low CSF glucose against age-specific percentiles.
  - reference: PMID:17718830

    explanation: Gives the pooled mean CSF glucose across 84 published cases.

genetic:
- name: SLC2A1
  gene_term:
    preferred_term: SLC2A1
    term:
      id: hgnc:11005
      label: SLC2A1
  association: Causative
  relationship_type: CAUSATIVE
  variant_origin: GERMLINE
  frequency: "About 90% de novo; about 10% inherited from a parent"
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:20301603
    reference_title: "Glucose Transporter Type 1 Deficiency Syndrome."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Glut1DS is most commonly caused by a heterozygous SLC2A1 pathogenic variant and inherited in an autosomal dominant manner. About 90% of individuals with Glut1DS have the disorder as the result of a de novo SLC2A1 pathogenic variant; about 10% of individuals have the disorder as the result of a pathogenic variant inherited from a parent."
    explanation: GeneReviews genetic counseling section quantifies the de novo versus inherited variant origin split.
  - reference: PMID:25487684
    reference_title: "Nationwide survey of glucose transporter-1 deficiency syndrome (GLUT-1DS) in Japan."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Pathological mutations in the SLC2A1 gene were identified in 28 out of 32 cases (87.5%)."
    explanation: Nationwide Japanese cohort documents the molecular diagnostic yield of SLC2A1 testing among clinically suspected patients.
  - reference: PMID:20129935
    reference_title: "Glucose transporter-1 deficiency syndrome: the expanding clinical and genetic spectrum of a treatable disorder."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "In these 57 patients we identified 49 different mutations, including six multiple exon deletions, six known mutations and 37 novel mutations (13 missense, five nonsense, 13 frame shift, four splice site and two translation initiation mutations)."
    explanation: Largest single-laboratory series enumerates the allelic spectrum, including multi-exon deletions detectable only by MLPA.
  - reference: PMID:9462754
    reference_title: "GLUT-1 deficiency syndrome caused by haploinsufficiency of the blood-brain barrier hexose carrier."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "We now report two distinct classes of mutations as the molecular basis for the functional defect of glucose transport: hemizygosity of GLUT1 and nonsense mutations resulting in truncation of the GLUT-1 protein."
    explanation: Founding gene-discovery report.
  - reference: PMID:15622525
    reference_title: "Glut-1 deficiency syndrome: clinical, genetic, and therapeutic aspects."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "We identified 16 rearrangements, including seven missense, one nonsense, one insertion, and seven deletion mutations. Fourteen were novel mutations. There were no obvious correlations between phenotype, genotype, or biochemical measures."
    explanation: Adds the deletion-heavy spectrum of a further 16 patients and the absence of genotype-phenotype correlation in that series.
  notes: >-
    Allelic spectrum: whole-gene hemizygosity, nonsense, frameshift, splice-site,
    translation-initiation, multi-exon deletion and missense alleles, all
    converging on haploinsufficiency of transport. Biallelic (compound
    heterozygous or homozygous) genotypes with asymptomatic heterozygous parents
    account for the rare recessive families. Whether variant class predicts
    severity is contested (see the genotype-phenotype discussion).
biochemical:
- name: Hypoglycorrhachia
  presence: Decreased
  context: >-
    Low CSF glucose relative to blood glucose (CSF:blood glucose ratio typically
    below 0.4) in the setting of normoglycemia is the classic diagnostic
    biochemical signature of GLUT1DS, reflecting impaired GLUT1-mediated glucose
    transport across the blood-brain barrier.
  biomarker_term:
    preferred_term: Hypoglycorrhachia
    term:
      id: HP:0011972
      label: Hypoglycorrhachia

    relationship: READOUT_OF
    direction: NEGATIVE
    endpoint_context: DIAGNOSTIC
    interpretation: >-
      A low CSF:blood glucose ratio reports reduced GLUT1-mediated glucose
      transport across the blood-brain barrier independent of systemic glucose
      levels.

      supports: SUPPORT

      explanation: Directly defines the diagnostic CSF:blood glucose ratio cutoff used clinically.

    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "The mean CSF/blood glucose ratio was 0.36 (0.28-0.48)."
    explanation: Nationwide cohort of confirmed cases reports the empirical mean and range of the diagnostic CSF:blood glucose ratio.
  - reference: PMID:12029447
    reference_title: "Facilitated glucose transporter protein type 1 (GLUT1) deficiency syndrome: impaired glucose transport into brain-- a review."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "The hallmark of the disease is a low glucose concentration in the CSF (hypoglycorrhachia) in the presence of normoglycaemia (CSF/blood glucose ratio <0.4)."
    explanation: Review confirms hypoglycorrhachia with CSF:blood glucose ratio below 0.4 as the diagnostic hallmark.
  - reference: PMID:20129935
    reference_title: "Glucose transporter-1 deficiency syndrome: the expanding clinical and genetic spectrum of a treatable disorder."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Cerebrospinal fluid glucose was below 2.5 mmol/l (range 0.9-2.4 mmol/l) in all patients and cerebrospinal fluid : blood glucose ratio was below 0.50 in all but one patient (range 0.19-0.52)."
    explanation: The ratio cut-off of 0.4 is not absolute; in 57 confirmed patients the ratio ranged up to 0.52, so a ratio below 0.5 with an absolute CSF glucose below 2.5 mmol/L is the practical screen.
  - reference: PMID:23999624
    reference_title: "Cerebrospinal fluid analysis in the workup of GLUT1 deficiency syndrome: a systematic review."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "The CSF to blood glucose ratios ranged from 0.19 to 0.59 and were at or below the 10th percentile for 139 of 152 patients (91%), but they could be within the normal range as well."
    explanation: Systematic review shows the ratio alone misses about 9% of genetically proven patients, so age-specific absolute CSF glucose percentiles should be used alongside it.
  - reference: PMID:18577546
    reference_title: "Paroxysmal exercise-induced dyskinesia and epilepsy is due to mutations in SLC2A1, encoding the glucose transporter GLUT1."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "A median CSF/blood glucose ratio of 0.52 (normal >0.60) in the patients and a reduced glucose uptake by mutated transporters compared with the wild-type as determined in Xenopus oocytes confirmed a pathogenic role of these mutations."
    explanation: In the milder PED phenotype the ratio is only modestly reduced (median 0.52), illustrating the graded relationship between residual transport and CSF glucose.
- name: CSF lactate
  presence: Low-to-normal
  context: >-
    CSF lactate is low-to-normal (mean about 1.0 mmol/L versus a normal mean of
    1.63 mmol/L; at or below the 10th percentile in 81% of reported patients)
    and is never elevated. Because raised CSF lactate is expected in
    mitochondrial encephalopathies, a low CSF lactate alongside low CSF glucose
    points to a transport rather than an oxidative defect.

    explanation: Quantifies the lowered CSF lactate in 16 patients.

    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "We conclude that if age-specific reference values are applied, CSF glucose and lactate levels are adequate biomarkers in the diagnostic workup of GLUT1 deficiency syndrome."
    explanation: Systematic review endorses CSF lactate, with glucose, as a diagnostic biomarker when age-specific references are used.
- name: Erythrocyte 3-O-methyl-D-glucose uptake
  presence: Decreased
  context: >-
    Zero-trans uptake of the non-metabolisable glucose analogue
    3-O-methyl-D-glucose into freshly isolated erythrocytes is reduced to about
    half of control Vmax with an unchanged Km. A cut-off of 74% of control
    uptake gives 99% sensitivity and 100% specificity against molecular
    confirmation, and residual uptake correlates with clinical severity, making
    it both a confirmatory functional test and a surrogate of the degree of
    haploinsufficiency.
  readouts:
  - target: Erythrocyte GLUT1 Dysfunction
    relationship: READOUT_OF
    direction: NEGATIVE
    endpoint_context: DIAGNOSTIC
    interpretation: >-
      Reduced uptake reports the transport capacity of the haploinsufficient
      GLUT1 population on the erythrocyte membrane.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:22190371
      reference_title: "Glut1 deficiency syndrome and erythrocyte glucose uptake assay."
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
      snippet: "These findings validate the erythrocyte glucose uptake assay as a confirmatory functional test for Glut1 DS and as a surrogate marker for GLUT1 haploinsufficiency."
      explanation: Establishes the assay as a surrogate of the transporter lesion.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:22190371
    reference_title: "Glut1 deficiency syndrome and erythrocyte glucose uptake assay."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "The cut-off for an abnormally low uptake value was increased from 60% to 74% with a corresponding sensitivity of 99% and specificity of 100%."
    explanation: Diagnostic performance of the uptake assay in 109 suspected cases.
  - reference: PMID:15622525
    reference_title: "Glut-1 deficiency syndrome: clinical, genetic, and therapeutic aspects."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: IN_VITRO
    snippet: "The mean V(max) for the 3-O-methyl-D-glucose uptake into erythrocytes was 996 fmol/10(6) red blood cells per second, significantly less (54 +/- 11%; t test, p < 0.05) than the mean control value of 1,847."
    explanation: Kinetic quantification of the roughly 50% reduction in transport capacity.
- name: Erythrocyte surface GLUT1 (METAglut1)
  presence: Decreased

diagnosis:
- name: Lumbar Puncture with Paired CSF and Blood Glucose
  description: >-
    Fasting lumbar puncture with simultaneous blood glucose measurement to
    demonstrate hypoglycorrhachia and calculate the CSF:blood glucose ratio,
    the key diagnostic biochemical test. CSF lactate should be measured on the
    same sample. Age-specific reference values, not a single ratio cut-off,
    should be applied.
  diagnosis_term:
    preferred_term: lumbar puncture with paired CSF and blood glucose
    term:
      id: NCIT:C15327
      label: Lumbar Puncture
  markers: CSF glucose, CSF:blood glucose ratio, CSF lactate
  results: >-
    CSF glucose typically below 2.5 mmol/L with a CSF:blood ratio below 0.5 and
    low-to-normal CSF lactate in the presence of normoglycemia.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:20129935
    reference_title: "Glucose transporter-1 deficiency syndrome: the expanding clinical and genetic spectrum of a treatable disorder."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Our study demonstrates that a lumbar puncture provides the diagnostic clue to glucose transporter-1 deficiency syndrome and can thereby dramatically reduce diagnostic delay to allow early start of the ketogenic diet."
    explanation: Series of 57 patients with a mean diagnostic delay of 6.6 years shows lumbar puncture as the step that shortens it.
  - reference: PMID:32913944
    reference_title: "Glut1 Deficiency Syndrome (Glut1DS): State of the art in 2020 and recommendations of the international Glut1DS study group."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    snippet: "Diagnosis is confirmed by the presence of these clinical signs, hypoglycorrhachia documented by lumbar puncture, and genetic analysis showing pathogenic SLC2A1 variants."
    explanation: Consensus diagnostic triad.
  - reference: PMID:23999624
    reference_title: "Cerebrospinal fluid analysis in the workup of GLUT1 deficiency syndrome: a systematic review."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "A typical CSF profile for GLUT1 deficiency syndrome, which is defined as a CSF glucose level at or below the 10th percentile, a CSF to blood glucose ratio at or below the 25th percentile, and a CSF lactate level at or below the 10th percentile, was found in only 35 of 4099 CSF samples (0.9%) present in our CSF database of patients who received a diagnosis other than GLUT1 deficiency syndrome."
    explanation: Specificity of the full CSF profile against a 4,099-sample database of other diagnoses.
  - reference: PMID:20301603
    reference_title: "Glucose Transporter Type 1 Deficiency Syndrome."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "The diagnosis of Glut1DS is established in a proband with suggestive clinical findings, hypoglycorrhachia documented by lumbar puncture, and a (usually) heterozygous pathogenic variant in SLC2A1 identified by molecular genetic testing."
    explanation: GeneReviews establishes lumbar puncture-documented hypoglycorrhachia as a core diagnostic criterion.
- name: SLC2A1 Molecular Genetic Testing
  description: >-
    Sequence analysis (and, if negative, gene-targeted deletion/duplication
    analysis such as MLPA) of SLC2A1 to identify the causative pathogenic
    variant. In the largest laboratory series 41% of referred requests were
    positive, and multi-exon deletions accounted for 6 of 49 distinct alleles,
    so deletion analysis is not optional.
  diagnosis_term:
    preferred_term: SLC2A1 sequence and deletion/duplication analysis
    term:
      id: NCIT:C19770
      label: Molecular Analysis
  markers: SLC2A1 pathogenic variant (heterozygous; rarely biallelic)
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:20129935
    reference_title: "Glucose transporter-1 deficiency syndrome: the expanding clinical and genetic spectrum of a treatable disorder."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "From 2004-2008, 132 requests for mutational analysis of the SLC2A1 gene were studied by automated Sanger sequencing and multiplex ligation-dependent probe amplification. Mutations in the SLC2A1 gene were detected in 54 patients (41%) and subsequently in three clinically affected family members."
    explanation: Defines the two-tier testing method and its yield in referred patients.
  - reference: PMID:25487684
    reference_title: "Nationwide survey of glucose transporter-1 deficiency syndrome (GLUT-1DS) in Japan."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Pathological mutations in the SLC2A1 gene were identified in 28 out of 32 cases (87.5%)."
    explanation: Nationwide cohort demonstrates the diagnostic yield of SLC2A1 molecular testing in clinically suspected cases.
- name: Erythrocyte GLUT1 Function Assay
  description: >-

    cut-off the uptake assay is 99% sensitive and 100% specific.

    explanation: Validation of the uptake assay's diagnostic performance in 109 suspected cases.

- name: METAglut1 Erythrocyte Surface GLUT1 Blood Test
  diagnosis_term:

    term:
      id: NCIT:C18020
      label: Diagnostic Procedure
  markers: Erythrocyte surface GLUT1 (flow cytometry)
  description: >-
    METAglut1 is a simple, noninvasive flow-cytometry blood test that
    quantifies GLUT1 protein on the surface of circulating erythrocytes,
    prospectively validated as a diagnostic test for GLUT1 deficiency syndrome

    explanation: Prospective multicenter validation reports the diagnostic sensitivity and specificity of the METAglut1 erythrocyte-surface GLUT1 blood test.
  - reference: PMID:37076312
    reference_title: "Prospective Multicenter Validation of a Simple Blood Test for the Diagnosis of Glut1 Deficiency Syndrome."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "METAglut1 succeeded to identify patients with Glut1DS with SCL2A1 mosaicism and variants of unknown significance."
    explanation: The blood test detects diagnostically challenging cases including SLC2A1 mosaicism and variants of unknown significance.
- name: Electroencephalography
  description: >-
    Routine and 24-hour EEG characterise seizure types and interictal
    discharges, but a normal interictal EEG is the most common finding at all
    ages, so EEG cannot exclude the diagnosis. A pre-prandial recording that
    improves after feeding is a supportive clue.
  diagnosis_term:
    preferred_term: electroencephalography
    term:
      id: NCIT:C38054
      label: Electroencephalography
  results: >-
    Normal interictal EEG most often; focal slowing or discharges in infants;
    generalized 2.5-4 Hz spike-wave from about 2 years, improving with food.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:12752470
    reference_title: "Seizure characterization and electroencephalographic features in Glut-1 deficiency syndrome."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "In all ages, a normal interictal EEG was the most common EEG finding."
    explanation: 32 continuous 24-hour EEG sessions and 42 routine EEGs establish the low sensitivity of EEG.
differential_diagnoses:
- name: Mitochondrial encephalopathies and other causes of low CSF glucose
  description: >-
    Low CSF glucose is more often seen acutely in CNS infection or malignancy
    than in a chronic neurological disorder, and in that chronic setting it is
    the diagnostic marker of Glut1DS. Mitochondrial encephalopathies can share
    seizures, movement disorder and developmental delay but characteristically
    raise CSF lactate, whereas in Glut1DS lactate is low-to-normal and blood
    glucose is normal.
  distinguishing_features:
  - Low CSF glucose with normal blood glucose and low-to-normal CSF lactate favours Glut1DS.
  - Raised CSF lactate favours a mitochondrial encephalopathy.
  - Acute presentation with pleocytosis or malignant cells points to infectious or neoplastic causes of low CSF glucose.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:24323444
    reference_title: "Child neurology: differential diagnosis of a low CSF glucose in children and young adults."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "We present a practical overview on the differential diagnosis of a low CSF glucose and stress the importance of recognizing a low CSF glucose as the diagnostic marker for GLUT1 deficiency syndrome, a treatable neurometabolic disorder."
    explanation: Frames low CSF glucose in chronic neurological disease as the Glut1DS marker to be separated from its other causes.
  - reference: PMID:17718830
    reference_title: "GLUT1 deficiency syndrome--2007 update."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "in the setting of normoglycaemia. CSF lactate is normal."
    explanation: Normal (never raised) CSF lactate is the discriminating feature from mitochondrial disease.
- name: Childhood absence epilepsy and other early-onset absence epilepsies
  description: >-
    Early-onset absence epilepsy without other features can be the sole
    presentation of an SLC2A1 variant; a Danish screen found SLC2A1 variants
    in 10% of absence-epilepsy patients, so Glut1DS should be excluded in
    absence epilepsy beginning before age four, especially with fasting or
    exertion sensitivity, developmental concerns or a movement disorder.
  distinguishing_features:
  - Onset of absences before age 4, drug resistance, developmental concerns, movement disorder or fasting/exertion sensitivity favour SLC2A1 testing.
  - Hypoglycorrhachia on lumbar puncture separates Glut1DS from idiopathic absence epilepsy.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:26537434
    reference_title: "The role of SLC2A1 mutations in myoclonic astatic epilepsy and absence epilepsy, and the estimated frequency of GLUT1 deficiency syndrome."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Mutations in SLC2A1 were detected in 5 (10%) of 50 patients with absence epilepsy, and in one (2.7%) of 37 patient with unselected epilepsies, ID, and/or various movement disorders."
    explanation: Quantifies the overlap between absence epilepsy and Glut1DS.
- name: Epilepsy with myoclonic-atonic seizures (Doose syndrome)
  description: >-
    Myoclonic-astatic epilepsy shares early-onset generalized seizures with
    Glut1DS, but a screen of 120 patients with myoclonic astatic epilepsy
    without associated movement disorder found no SLC2A1 variants.
  distinguishing_features:
  - Absence of a movement disorder and normal CSF glucose argue against Glut1DS in myoclonic-atonic epilepsy.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:26537434
    reference_title: "The role of SLC2A1 mutations in myoclonic astatic epilepsy and absence epilepsy, and the estimated frequency of GLUT1 deficiency syndrome."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "None of the 120 MAE patients harbored SLC2A1 mutations."
    explanation: Negative screen separates typical MAE from Glut1DS.
- name: Other paroxysmal dyskinesias (PRRT2-related kinesigenic and non-kinesigenic dyskinesias)
  description: >-
    Paroxysmal dyskinesias are classified by trigger; the exertion-induced
    (PED) form, provoked by prolonged exercise or fasting rather than sudden
    movement, is the one most likely to be Glut1DS, and a fasting or exercise
    trigger is the diagnostic clue.
  distinguishing_features:
  - Episodes provoked by prolonged exertion or fasting, lasting minutes to an hour and affecting mainly the legs, favour SLC2A1-related PED.
  - Brief attacks provoked by sudden movement favour PRRT2-related kinesigenic dyskinesia.
  - A reduced CSF:blood glucose ratio (median about 0.5 in PED families) supports Glut1DS.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:23443458
    reference_title: "Phenotypic spectrum of glucose transporter type 1 deficiency syndrome (Glut1 DS)."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "A clue to the diagnosis in patients with paroxysmal symptoms may be the triggering of episodes during fasting or exercise."
    explanation: Trigger profile as the discriminating clinical feature.
  - reference: PMID:18451999
    reference_title: "GLUT1 mutations are a cause of paroxysmal exertion-induced dyskinesias and induce hemolytic anemia by a cation leak."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Paroxysmal dyskinesias are episodic movement disorders that can be inherited or are sporadic in nature."
    explanation: Places SLC2A1-related PED within the broader paroxysmal dyskinesia group from which it must be distinguished.
treatments:
- name: Ketogenic Diet Therapy
  description: >-
    Age-specific ketogenic diet therapies (KDTs) supply ketone bodies as an
    alternative fuel for brain energy metabolism, bypassing the GLUT1 transport
    defect. Earlier initiation, ideally in infancy, is associated with better
    seizure control and long-term neurologic outcome. Effective for both
    seizure control and the movement disorder.
  action_category: THERAPEUTIC
  therapeutic_modality: BEHAVIORAL
  treatment_term:
    preferred_term: ketogenic diet intake
    term:
      id: NCIT:C173168
      label: Ketogenic Diet
  target_phenotypes:
  - preferred_term: Seizure
    term:
      id: HP:0001250
      label: Seizure
  - preferred_term: Paroxysmal dyskinesia
    term:
      id: HP:0007166
      label: Paroxysmal dyskinesia
  target_mechanisms:
  - target: Cerebral Glucose Energy Deficit
    treatment_effect: BYPASSES
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:20301603
      reference_title: "Glucose Transporter Type 1 Deficiency Syndrome."
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
      snippet: "Targeted therapy: Age-specific KDTs primarily provide a supplemental fuel, namely, ketone bodies, for brain energy metabolism."
      explanation: GeneReviews describes the ketogenic diet mechanism of action as supplying an alternative cerebral fuel that bypasses the GLUT1 transport defect.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:36303089
    reference_title: "Ketogenic diet therapy in children with epilepsy caused by SLC2A1 mutations: a single-center single-arm retrospective study."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "All patients were seizure-free within a month of receiving the diet therapy. All patients were followed up for six months, three were followed up for 12 months after the treatment, and there was no recurrence of epilepsy during this period."
    explanation: Retrospective single-center cohort of SLC2A1-mutation patients demonstrates rapid and durable seizure control with ketogenic diet therapy.
  - reference: PMID:20301603
    reference_title: "Glucose Transporter Type 1 Deficiency Syndrome."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Typically, the earlier the treatment the better the long-term clinical outcome."
    explanation: GeneReviews documents that earlier ketogenic diet initiation yields better long-term neurologic outcome.
  - reference: PMID:20129935
    reference_title: "Glucose transporter-1 deficiency syndrome: the expanding clinical and genetic spectrum of a treatable disorder."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "a ketogenic diet was effective in most of the patients with epilepsy (86%) and also reduced movement disorders in 48% of the patients with a classical phenotype and 71% of the patients with a non-classical phenotype."
    explanation: Quantifies response of both seizures and movement disorder in 57 confirmed patients.
  - reference: PMID:26803281
    reference_title: "Use of dietary therapies amongst patients with GLUT1 deficiency syndrome."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Of those with seizures, 95% of the children had >50% seizure reduction and 80% had >90% seizure reduction."
    explanation: Largest (92-family, parent-reported) series of dietary therapy in Glut1DS.
  - reference: PMID:17718830
    reference_title: "GLUT1 deficiency syndrome--2007 update."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "The ketogenic diet is the treatment of choice as it provides an alternative fuel to the brain. It should be introduced early and maintained into puberty. Seizures are effectively controlled with the onset of ketosis, but might recur and require comedication."
    explanation: Review of 84 cases states duration guidance and the possibility of seizure recurrence needing co-medication.
  - reference: PMID:24789115
    reference_title: "Long-term clinical course of Glut1 deficiency syndrome."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Earlier introduction of the ketogenic diet correlated with better long-term outcomes on some measures."
    explanation: Longitudinal cohort (mean 14 years) supports the early-treatment claim with outcome data rather than expert statement.
  - reference: PMID:32913944
    reference_title: "Glut1 Deficiency Syndrome (Glut1DS): State of the art in 2020 and recommendations of the international Glut1DS study group."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    snippet: "KDT represent standard choices with Glut1DS-specific recommendations regarding duration, composition, and management."
    explanation: International consensus designates KDT as standard of care.
- name: Modified Atkins Diet and other ketogenic dietary variants
  description: >-
    Less restrictive ketogenic dietary therapies (modified Atkins diet,
    medium-chain-triglyceride diet, low-glycaemic-index treatment) are used,
    particularly in older children and adults, and in a large family survey
    gave seizure-freedom rates comparable to the classic 4:1 diet. They act by
    the same ketone-supply mechanism as the classic diet.
  action_category: THERAPEUTIC
  therapeutic_modality: BEHAVIORAL
  treatment_term:
    preferred_term: modified Atkins and other ketogenic dietary therapies
    term:
      id: NCIT:C15447
      label: Dietary Intervention
  target_mechanisms:
  - target: Cerebral Glucose Energy Deficit
    treatment_effect: BYPASSES
    description: Supplies ketone bodies as an alternative cerebral fuel, as the classic ketogenic diet does.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:26803281
    reference_title: "Use of dietary therapies amongst patients with GLUT1 deficiency syndrome."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Diets used were extremely varied: 59 were treated with the classic ketogenic diet (KD), 29 with the Modified Atkins Diet (MAD), 4 with the Medium-chain Triglyceride (MCT) Diet and 2 with the low glycemic index treatment."
    explanation: Documents real-world use of the dietary variants.
  - reference: PMID:26803281
    reference_title: "Use of dietary therapies amongst patients with GLUT1 deficiency syndrome."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "There was an equal percentage of children seizure-free receiving the KD/MCT Diets compared to the MAD/Low Glycemic Index Treatment (74% vs. 63%, p=0.30)."
    explanation: No significant difference in seizure freedom between classic and less restrictive diets in this parent-reported survey.
- name: Triheptanoin
  description: >-
    Triheptanoin is an anaplerotic odd-chain (C7) medium-chain triglyceride
    that provides Krebs-cycle substrates and an alternative brain fuel
    independent of GLUT1. An open-label study reported marked reduction of
    non-epileptic paroxysmal manifestations and normalized brain bioenergetics,
    but a subsequent randomized placebo-controlled trial did not show a
    significant reduction in seizure frequency in patients not on a ketogenic
    diet, leaving its efficacy for the epilepsy phenotype unproven.
  treatment_term:
    preferred_term: Pharmacotherapy
    term:
      id: NCIT:C15986
      label: Pharmacotherapy
    therapeutic_agent:
    - preferred_term: Triheptanoin
      term:
        id: CHEBI:757060
        label: triheptanoin
  action_category: THERAPEUTIC
  therapeutic_modality: SMALL_MOLECULE
  target_mechanisms:
  - target: Cerebral Glucose Energy Deficit
    treatment_effect: BYPASSES
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:26536893
      reference_title: "Triheptanoin dramatically reduces paroxysmal motor disorder in patients with GLUT1 deficiency."
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
      snippet: "Treatment with triheptanoin resulted in a 90% clinical improvement in non-epileptic paroxysmal manifestations and a normalised brain bioenergetics profile in patients with GLUT1-DS."
      explanation: Open-label study demonstrates triheptanoin supplies anaplerotic Krebs-cycle substrates that bypass the GLUT1 defect and normalize brain energy metabolism.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:26536893
    reference_title: "Triheptanoin dramatically reduces paroxysmal motor disorder in patients with GLUT1 deficiency."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Treatment with triheptanoin resulted in a 90% clinical improvement in non-epileptic paroxysmal manifestations and a normalised brain bioenergetics profile in patients with GLUT1-DS."
    explanation: Open-label pilot study supports triheptanoin efficacy against the non-epileptic paroxysmal (movement-disorder) manifestations of GLUT1DS.
  - reference: PMID:35441706
    reference_title: "A randomized, double-blind trial of triheptanoin for drug-resistant epilepsy in glucose transporter 1 deficiency syndrome."
    supports: REFUTE
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Triheptanoin did not significantly reduce seizure frequency in patients with Glut1DS not on the ketogenic diet."
    explanation: Randomized double-blind placebo-controlled trial qualifies the treatment claim by showing no significant seizure-frequency benefit for the epilepsy phenotype.
- name: Anti-Seizure Medications to Avoid
  description: >-
    In individuals on ketogenic diet therapy, valproic acid should be avoided
    because it increases the risk of a Reye-like illness and may also inhibit
    glucose transport; phenobarbital, acetazolamide, topiramate, and zonisamide
    may be relatively contraindicated as adjunctive treatment. This is a
    "circumstance to avoid" (GeneReviews Agents/Circumstances to Avoid section)
    rather than a recommended treatment; retained here as a treatment-adjacent
    drug-safety note.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:20301603
    reference_title: "Glucose Transporter Type 1 Deficiency Syndrome."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Agents/circumstances to avoid: In individuals on KDTs: (1) avoidance of treatment of seizures with valproic acid, because it increases the risk of a Reye-like illness and may also inhibit glucose transport; (2) other anti-seizure medications (ASMs) including phenobarbital, acetazolamide, topiramate, and zonisamide may be relatively contraindicated as adjunctive treatment."
    explanation: GeneReviews Agents/Circumstances to Avoid section directly documents these drug-safety warnings for GLUT1DS patients on ketogenic diet therapy.
- name: Multidisciplinary Supportive Care
  description: >-
    Physical medicine and rehabilitation, physical therapy, occupational
    therapy, speech and language therapy, educational programs, and clinical
    genetics/genetic counseling, alongside routine neurology follow-up to
    monitor response to ketogenic diet therapy and identify new manifestations.
  treatment_term:
    preferred_term: Supportive Care
    term:
      id: NCIT:C15747
      label: Supportive Care

  - reference: PMID:20301603

    supports: SUPPORT

    snippet: "Supportive care: In addition to educational programs to address the individual's needs, multidisciplinary care by specialists in neurology familiar with KDTs, physical medicine and rehabilitation, physical therapy, occupational therapy, speech and therapy, and clinical genetics and genetic counseling."
    explanation: GeneReviews management section documents the multidisciplinary supportive care regimen recommended alongside ketogenic diet therapy.
- name: Genetic Counseling
  description: >-
    Each child of an individual with autosomal dominant Glut1DS has a 50% chance
    of inheriting the SLC2A1 variant; because about 90% of cases are de novo
    and transmitting parents may be mildly affected or unaffected (possible
    mosaicism), parental testing is informative. Prenatal and preimplantation
    testing are possible once the familial variant is known.
  action_category: COUNSELING_INFORMATIONAL
  treatment_term:
    preferred_term: Genetic Counseling
    term:
      id: NCIT:C15240
      label: Genetic Counseling
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:20301603
    reference_title: "Glucose Transporter Type 1 Deficiency Syndrome."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Each child of an individual with autosomal dominant Glut1DS has a 50% chance of inheriting the SLC2A1 pathogenic variant and being clinically affected."
    explanation: Recurrence risk for offspring of an affected individual.
  - reference: PMID:20301603
    reference_title: "Glucose Transporter Type 1 Deficiency Syndrome."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Once the SLC2A1 pathogenic variant(s) have been identified in an affected family member, prenatal and preimplantation genetic testing for Glut1DS are possible."
    explanation: Reproductive options.
- name: Evaluation of At-Risk Relatives and Early Treatment
  description: >-
    Because early ketogenic diet therapy improves seizure control and long-term
    neurological outcome, at-risk newborns, infants and other relatives of a
    proband should be tested for the familial SLC2A1 variant so that treatment
    can begin before or at the first manifestations.
  action_category: SCREENING
  treatment_term:

    term:

    explanation: GeneReviews recommendation to evaluate relatives at risk.
  - reference: PMID:20301603

    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL

    explanation: The rationale for presymptomatic identification.
- name: Neurological Surveillance
  description: >-
    Routinely scheduled neurology review to judge response to ketogenic diet
    therapy and to detect new manifestations, especially the movement disorder
    that emerges in later childhood and adolescence, with follow-up by the
    treating physical, occupational and speech-language therapists.

- name: AAV9-Mediated GLUT1 Gene Replacement (Experimental)
  description: >-
    Experimental, preclinical only. In Glut1-deficient mice, pre-symptomatic
    AAV9-mediated repletion of GLUT1 averts the brain microvascular defect and
    prevents disease, and repletion in early-symptomatic animals still
    restores the microvasculature and ameliorates disease, whereas adult
    repletion gives no benefit. The 2020 consensus notes that interventions to
    restore GLUT1 protein content and function are in development. No human
    trial of GLUT1 gene replacement is cited here.
  action_category: THERAPEUTIC
  therapeutic_modality: GENE_THERAPY
  treatment_term:
    preferred_term: AAV9 SLC2A1 gene replacement

      id: NCIT:C15238
      label: Gene Therapy

    treatment_effect: RESTORES
    description: Vector-delivered SLC2A1 restores GLUT1 protein content in the deficient brain.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:32913944
      reference_title: "Glut1 Deficiency Syndrome (Glut1DS): State of the art in 2020 and recommendations of the international Glut1DS study group."
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: OTHER
      snippet: "Ongoing research has identified future interventions to restore Glut1 protein content and function."
      explanation: Consensus statement records protein-restoring interventions as the direction of therapeutic research.
  - target: Arrested Cerebral Angiogenesis and Microvascular Diminution
    treatment_effect: RESTORES
    description: Early GLUT1 repletion restores the cerebral microvasculature in model mice.
    evidence:

      supports: SUPPORT

      snippet: "Glut1 repletion in early-symptomatic mutants that have experienced sustained periods of low brain glucose nevertheless restores the cerebral microvasculature and ameliorates disease."
      explanation: Direct rescue of the vascular node by gene replacement in symptomatic mice.

    supports: SUPPORT

datasets: []
discussions:
- discussion_id: gap_glut1_energy_deficit_to_hyperexcitability_intermediate
  prompt: >-
    Which cellular intermediate translates chronic cerebral glucose
    under-supply into the specific pattern of neuronal hyperexcitability and
    hypersynchrony seen in Glut1DS — failure of the astrocyte-neuron lactate
    shuttle, selective energy vulnerability of fast-spiking GABAergic
    interneurons, disturbed adenosine/thalamocortical signaling, or a
    combination?
  kind: KNOWLEDGE_GAP
  status: OPEN
  attaches_to:
  - pathophysiology#Cerebral Glucose Energy Deficit
  - pathophysiology#Neuronal Hyperexcitability and Hypersynchrony
  rationale: >-
    The causal edge from "Cerebral Glucose Energy Deficit" to "Neuronal
    Hyperexcitability and Hypersynchrony" is curated as
    INDIRECT_KNOWN_INTERMEDIATES, but the identity of that intermediate is not
    actually established. Glucose reaches the brain almost exclusively through
    GLUT1, yet why a global fuel shortfall produces the particular Glut1DS
    electroclinical signature (including 2.5-4 Hz generalized spike-wave and
    absence-like seizures) rather than diffuse depression is unresolved.
    Candidate mechanisms — astrocytic lactate-supply failure, preferential ATP
    starvation of parvalbumin interneurons whose high firing rates make them
    metabolically expensive, and adenosine-mediated network effects — carry
    different therapeutic implications, so resolving the intermediate would
    materially sharpen the mechanism model.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:20301603
    reference_title: "Glucose Transporter Type 1 Deficiency Syndrome."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Glucose, the essential metabolic fuel for the brain, is transported into the brain exclusively by the protein glucose transporter type 1 (Glut1) across the endothelial cells forming the blood-brain barrier (BBB)."
    explanation: >-
      Establishes the exclusive GLUT1 route for cerebral glucose, framing why
      the deficit is global; the open question is which cell type first
      converts that global shortfall into focal-onset hyperexcitability.
  - reference: PMID:16497725
    reference_title: "A mouse model for Glut-1 haploinsufficiency."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    snippet: "GLUT-1+/- mice have epileptiform discharges on electroencephalography (EEG), impaired motor activity, incoordination, hypoglycorrhachia, microencephaly, decreased brain glucose uptake as measured by positron emission tomography (PET) scan and decreased brain Glut-1 expression by western blot (66%)."
    explanation: >-
      Provides a tractable in vivo system in which the epileptiform phenotype is
      reproduced, i.e., a model where the missing cellular intermediate could be
      dissected with cell-type-specific readouts.
  proposed_experiments:
  - experiment_id: exp_glut1_celltype_metabolic_intermediate
    name: Cell-type-resolved metabolic-to-excitability mapping in Glut1 haploinsufficiency
    description: >-
      In GLUT-1+/- mice and patient-derived iPSC neuron-astrocyte co-cultures,
      simultaneously measure astrocytic lactate output, interneuron-specific
      ATP/metabolic stress, and network excitability, then test whether
      interneuron-targeted metabolic rescue (or lactate supplementation)
      normalizes hypersynchrony independently of restoring bulk neuronal
      glucose.
    experiment_type:
      preferred_term: cell-type-resolved metabolic and excitability mapping experiment
    perturbations:
    - name: Interneuron-selective metabolic rescue
      target: pathophysiology#Cerebral Glucose Energy Deficit
      description: >-
        Selectively supply alternative fuel (lactate/ketone) or restore GLUT1 in
        astrocytes vs interneurons vs principal neurons to identify the
        rate-limiting compartment.
    readouts:
    - name: Network hyperexcitability
      target: pathophysiology#Neuronal Hyperexcitability and Hypersynchrony
      assays:
      - preferred_term: multielectrode array recording
      - preferred_term: electroencephalography
      direction: POSITIVE
    controls:
    - name: Wild-type co-cultures
      description: GLUT1-normal neuron-astrocyte co-cultures under matched glucose conditions.
    decision_criterion: >-
      An intermediate is supported if rescuing a single cell compartment (e.g.,
      astrocytic lactate supply or interneuron energetics) normalizes network
      excitability while leaving bulk neuronal glucose uptake unchanged.
    would_support:
    - pathophysiology#Neuronal Hyperexcitability and Hypersynchrony

- discussion_id: gap_glut1_ketogenic_seizure_vs_neurodevelopment_dissociation
  prompt: >-
    Why does ketone-based fuel substitution robustly control seizures in Glut1DS
    yet only partially rescue the movement disorder and cognitive impairment —
    is the residual deficit a missed developmental window, regional or
    functional under-supply of ketone-derived fuel, or a non-fuel role of
    glucose that ketones cannot replace?
  kind: KNOWLEDGE_GAP
  status: OPEN
  attaches_to:
  - pathophysiology#Cerebral Glucose Energy Deficit
  - pathophysiology#Impaired Neurodevelopment and Movement Disorder
  - pathophysiology#Recurrent Pharmacoresistant Seizures
  rationale: >-
    Ketogenic diet therapy is the mainstay treatment and supplies ketone bodies
    as an alternative cerebral fuel that bypasses the GLUT1 transport defect. In
    practice it controls seizures far more completely than it reverses the
    paroxysmal movement disorder, ataxia/dystonia, or cognitive outcome, and
    outcomes are consistently better with earlier treatment. Whether the
    residual, seizure-independent morbidity reflects irreversible injury
    incurred before diagnosis (a developmental-window effect), inadequate
    ketone delivery to specific circuits, or a role of glucose beyond bulk fuel
    (e.g., biosynthetic/pentose-phosphate demands) is unresolved — and it
    determines whether earlier or supplemental (e.g., anaplerotic) therapy could
    close the gap.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:20301603
    reference_title: "Glucose Transporter Type 1 Deficiency Syndrome."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Targeted therapy: Age-specific KDTs primarily provide a supplemental fuel, namely, ketone bodies, for brain energy metabolism."
    explanation: >-
      Establishes that ketogenic therapy acts by fuel substitution, framing the
      question of why fuel substitution rescues seizures more completely than
      neurodevelopmental outcomes.
  - reference: PMID:20301603
    reference_title: "Glucose Transporter Type 1 Deficiency Syndrome."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Typically, the earlier the treatment the better the long-term clinical outcome."
    explanation: >-
      Documents the developmental-window dependence of outcome, one of the
      competing explanations for the residual neurodevelopmental deficit.
  - reference: PMID:28106060
    reference_title: "Brain microvasculature defects and Glut1 deficiency syndrome averted by early repletion of the glucose transporter-1 protein."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    snippet: "Restoring the protein to 2-week old mutants, in which certain disease characteristics are readily apparent, is less effective in shaping normal brain microvasculature."
    explanation: >-
      Model-organism evidence that later restoration incompletely reverses
      structural brain defects, supporting a developmental-window contribution
      to residual, seizure-independent morbidity.
  - reference: PMID:17718830
    reference_title: "GLUT1 deficiency syndrome--2007 update."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "The effect on neurodevelopment appears less impressive."
    explanation: >-
      Review of 84 cases records the seizure-versus-neurodevelopment
      dissociation that this gap is about.
  - reference: PMID:40405536
    reference_title: "Glut1 Deficiency Syndrome: Novel Pathomechanisms, Current Concepts, and Challenges."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    snippet: "KDT are ineffective in some patients for reasons yet unknown."
    explanation: >-
      2025 review states that the determinants of KDT non-response are unknown,
      confirming the gap remains open.
  proposed_experiments:
  - experiment_id: exp_glut1_treatment_timing_endpoint_dissociation
    name: Treatment-timing dissociation of seizure vs neurodevelopmental endpoints
    description: >-
      In GLUT-1+/- mice, initiate ketogenic therapy or GLUT1 repletion across a
      graded set of ages and measure seizure/EEG control separately from motor
      coordination, brain growth, and cognitive-behavioral endpoints, testing
      whether the neurodevelopmental endpoints have an earlier closing window
      than the seizure endpoint.
    experiment_type:
      preferred_term: treatment-timing endpoint dissociation experiment
    perturbations:
    - name: Age-graded ketogenic/repletion therapy
      target: pathophysiology#Cerebral Glucose Energy Deficit
      description: Start fuel substitution or GLUT1 repletion at several developmental ages.
    readouts:
    - name: Seizure control
      target: pathophysiology#Recurrent Pharmacoresistant Seizures
      assays:
      - preferred_term: electroencephalography
      direction: NEGATIVE
    - name: Motor and neurodevelopmental outcome
      target: pathophysiology#Impaired Neurodevelopment and Movement Disorder
      assays:
      - preferred_term: behavioral assay
      direction: POSITIVE
    controls:
    - name: Untreated mutants and wild-type
      description: Matched untreated GLUT-1+/- and wild-type animals.
    decision_criterion: >-
      A developmental-window explanation is supported if neurodevelopmental
      endpoints lose responsiveness at an earlier treatment age than seizure
      control does; a fuel-limitation explanation is supported if both endpoints
      respond equally whenever adequate alternative fuel is supplied.
    would_support:
    - pathophysiology#Impaired Neurodevelopment and Movement Disorder

- discussion_id: gap_glut1_mouse_model_paroxysmal_phenotype_fidelity
  prompt: >-
    Does the GLUT-1+/- mouse faithfully model the full human Glut1DS phenotype —
    in particular the paroxysmal features (paroxysmal eye-head movements,
    exercise-induced dyskinesia) and the spectrum extending to early-onset
    absence epilepsy and movement-disorder-predominant presentations — or does
    it capture mainly the core seizure/hypoglycorrhachia/microcephaly triad?
  kind: HUMAN_MODEL_MISMATCH
  status: OPEN
  attaches_to:
  - pathophysiology#Neuronal Hyperexcitability and Hypersynchrony
  - pathophysiology#Impaired Neurodevelopment and Movement Disorder
  rationale: >-
    The GLUT-1+/- mouse reproduces epileptiform EEG, motor impairment,
    hypoglycorrhachia and microencephaly and is described as mimicking the
    classical human presentation, making it the workhorse model for Glut1DS.
    But the human disorder is defined as much by paroxysmal, activity- and
    fasting-triggered phenomena and by a wide phenotypic spectrum (paroxysmal
    exercise-induced dyskinesia, early-onset absence epilepsy, isolated
    movement disorder) that depend on human-specific behavioral repertoire and
    developmental glucose-demand timing. Whether these paroxysmal and
    spectrum-defining features are recapitulated — and thus whether the model
    can validate therapies aimed at them — is the open translational question,
    distinct from a claim that evidence is simply absent.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:16497725
    reference_title: "A mouse model for Glut-1 haploinsufficiency."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    snippet: "The GLUT-1+/- murine phenotype mimics the classical human presentation of Glut-1 DS."
    explanation: >-
      Documents fidelity to the classical core phenotype; the open question is
      whether the paroxysmal and spectrum-defining human features are also
      captured.
  proposed_experiments:
  - experiment_id: exp_glut1_paroxysmal_phenotype_provocation
    name: Provocation phenotyping of paroxysmal features in Glut1 models
    description: >-
      Deep-phenotype GLUT-1+/- mice and patient-derived neuronal/BBB models
      under physiological provocations (fasting, exertion, post-prandial state)
      to test for movement-triggered dyskinesia-like events and absence-like
      spike-wave, comparing directly against the documented human paroxysmal
      spectrum.
    experiment_type:
      preferred_term: provocation phenotyping experiment
    readouts:
    - name: Paroxysmal movement and absence-like events
      target: pathophysiology#Impaired Neurodevelopment and Movement Disorder
      assays:
      - preferred_term: behavioral assay
      - preferred_term: electroencephalography
      direction: POSITIVE
    controls:
    - name: Wild-type littermates under identical provocation
      description: Matched wild-type animals exposed to the same fasting/exertion protocols.
    decision_criterion: >-
      Human fidelity for the paroxysmal spectrum is supported if physiological
      provocations reproducibly elicit movement-triggered dyskinesia-like events
      and absence-like discharges in mutants but not controls; absence of such
      events flags a genuine human/model mismatch for these features.
    would_support:
    - pathophysiology#Impaired Neurodevelopment and Movement Disorder

- discussion_id: controversy_glut1_genotype_phenotype_correlation
  prompt: >-
    Does SLC2A1 variant class (truncating or deletion versus missense) predict
    clinical severity in Glut1DS, or is severity set only by residual
    transporter activity irrespective of variant class?
  kind: CONTROVERSY
  status: OPEN
  attaches_to:
  - pathophysiology#SLC2A1 Loss-of-Function Variant
  - genetic#SLC2A1
  - has_subtypes#
  rationale: >-
    The largest laboratory series reported that mutation type was related to
    the severity of intellectual disability and to the presence of complex
    movement disorders, whereas the Columbia series of 16 new patients and the
    2007 review of 84 published cases found no correlation between genotype,
    phenotype and biochemical measures, and the French national cohort found
    overlapping phenotypes that resist genotype-phenotype assignment. What
    does correlate across series is residual erythrocyte glucose uptake, which
    even determines whether a family presents as dominant or recessive. The
    resolution matters for counselling and for how the has_subtypes entries are
    read: they are phenotype clusters on a continuum, not variant-defined
    entities.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:20129935
    reference_title: "Glucose transporter-1 deficiency syndrome: the expanding clinical and genetic spectrum of a treatable disorder."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Type of mutation was related to the severity of mental retardation and the presence of complex movement disorders."
    explanation: Supports the claim that variant class predicts severity, in 57 genetically confirmed patients.
  - reference: PMID:15622525
    reference_title: "Glut-1 deficiency syndrome: clinical, genetic, and therapeutic aspects."
    supports: REFUTE
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "There were no obvious correlations between phenotype, genotype, or biochemical measures."
    explanation: Refutes the claim in an independent series of 16 patients.
  - reference: PMID:17718830
    reference_title: "GLUT1 deficiency syndrome--2007 update."
    supports: REFUTE
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Autosomal dominant transmission and several mutational hot spots have been identified, but phenotype-genotype correlations are not yet apparent."
    explanation: Review of 84 published cases found no apparent correlation.
  - reference: PMID:26193382
    reference_title: "From splitting GLUT1 deficiency syndromes to overlapping phenotypes."
    supports: REFUTE
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "thus making it difficult to identify definite genotype-phenotype correlations"
    explanation: French national cohort argues phenotypes overlap along a continuum rather than segregating by genotype.
  - reference: PMID:22190371
    reference_title: "Glut1 deficiency syndrome and erythrocyte glucose uptake assay."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "We also noted a significant correlation between the mean erythrocyte 3-OMG uptake and clinical severity (R(2) = 0.94)."
    explanation: Supports the alternative position that residual transporter function, rather than variant class, predicts severity.
mechanistic_hypotheses:
- hypothesis_group_id: canonical_glut1_energy_deficit
  hypothesis_label: Blood-brain-barrier glucose transport failure causes cerebral energy deficit
  status: CANONICAL
  description: >-
    Haploinsufficient GLUT1 at the brain endothelium limits glucose delivery,
    producing hypoglycorrhachia and a chronic brain energy deficit that is
    greatest when developmental glucose demand peaks; the deficit drives
    cortical hyperexcitability, impaired neurodevelopment and, under exertion
    or fasting, episodic basal ganglia failure. Ketogenic diet therapy works by
    supplying ketone bodies that bypass the transporter.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:32913944
    reference_title: "Glut1 Deficiency Syndrome (Glut1DS): State of the art in 2020 and recommendations of the international Glut1DS study group."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    snippet: "Glut1 deficiency syndrome (Glut1DS) is a brain energy failure syndrome caused by impaired glucose transport across brain tissue barriers."
    explanation: Consensus definition of the canonical mechanism.
  - reference: PMID:9462754
    reference_title: "GLUT-1 deficiency syndrome caused by haploinsufficiency of the blood-brain barrier hexose carrier."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "These symptoms suggested the existence of a defect in glucose transport across the blood brain barrier."
    explanation: The founding formulation of the hypothesis, confirmed by the mutations reported in the same paper.
- hypothesis_group_id: microvascular_developmental_defect
  hypothesis_label: GLUT1 deficiency arrests cerebral angiogenesis, adding a structural microvascular deficit
  status: EMERGING
  description: >-
    Beyond its fuel-transport role, endothelial GLUT1 is required for postnatal
    cerebral angiogenesis; in model mice low GLUT1 arrests microvessel growth
    and early, but not late, repletion averts disease. The consensus statement
    has adopted impaired microvascular development as a complication of delayed
    treatment, but the human contribution is unmeasured, and this model may
    partly explain why fuel substitution rescues seizures more completely than
    cognition.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:28106060
    reference_title: "Brain microvasculature defects and Glut1 deficiency syndrome averted by early repletion of the glucose transporter-1 protein."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    snippet: "We used model mice to demonstrate that low Glut1 protein arrests cerebral angiogenesis, resulting in a profound diminution of the brain microvasculature without compromising the blood-brain barrier."
    explanation: Primary mouse evidence for the hypothesis.
  - reference: PMID:40405536
    reference_title: "Glut1 Deficiency Syndrome: Novel Pathomechanisms, Current Concepts, and Challenges."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    snippet: "Research reaches beyond the concept of brain energy depletion by impaired GLUT1-mediated glucose transfer across the blood-brain barrier."
    explanation: 2025 review frames non-fuel mechanisms as an active, still-emerging area.
- hypothesis_group_id: erythrocyte_cation_leak_hemolysis
  hypothesis_label: Pore-region GLUT1 alleles cause hemolysis through a cation leak
  status: EMERGING
  description: >-
    Specific pore-region SLC2A1 alleles render the mutant transporter permeable
    to sodium, potassium and calcium; the resulting erythrocyte electrolyte
    disturbance produces echinocytosis and hemolytic anemia. Alleles that only
    reduce transport do not cause hemolysis, so this is an allele-specific gain
    of an abnormal function layered on haploinsufficiency, established in one
    family plus heterologous expression.
  applies_to_subtypes:
  - GLUT1 DS with Paroxysmal Exertion-Induced Dyskinesia
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:18451999
    reference_title: "GLUT1 mutations are a cause of paroxysmal exertion-induced dyskinesias and induce hemolytic anemia by a cation leak."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: IN_VITRO
    snippet: "the hemolysis with echinocytosis may result from alterations in intracellular electrolytes caused by a cation leak through mutant GLUT1"
    explanation: The authors' mechanistic proposal from oocyte and erythrocyte studies.

- name: Glut1 Rgsc200 ENU missense mouse

    ameliorates disease, whereas adult repletion does not.

    explanation: The authors' translational conclusion from this rescue model, which is why the model is recorded here.

notes: >-
  Lump/split decision (2026-09-05 revision, claim issue #11080). This entry is
  the single Disease record for the SLC2A1 haploinsufficiency spectrum and is
  now anchored to the umbrella term MONDO:0000188, GLUT1 deficiency syndrome.
  Its two MONDO descendants, MONDO:0011724 (encephalopathy due to GLUT1
  deficiency, the classic infantile form) and MONDO:0012805 (childhood-onset
  GLUT1 deficiency syndrome 2, paroxysmal exercise-induced dyskinesia), are
  modelled as has_subtypes rather than as separate entries, because they share
  one molecular lesion and one mechanism chain and differ only in residual
  transporter activity and hence severity; the French national cohort documents
  individual patients crossing between them over time. The earlier version of
  this entry, created 2026-07-06, anchored disease_term to MONDO:0011724, which
  left the umbrella concept uncurated; the re-anchoring retires
  stubs/GLUT1_Deficiency_Syndrome.yaml. Dystonia 9 (DYT9, MONDO:0010983,
  OMIM 601042) was initially listed as a third subtype for completeness of the
  OMIM phenotypic series. It has been folded into the paroxysmal
  exertion-induced dyskinesia subtype instead, because Weber and colleagues
  sequenced SLC2A1 in the original German/Dutch DYT9 pedigree and concluded
  that DYT9 and DYT18 are allelic disorders; listing both as sibling subtypes
  would double-count one concept. MONDO:0010983 is therefore deliberately not
  bound as a subtype_term, and the DYT9 presentation is described within the
  DYT18 subtype, whose description records the additional spastic paraparesis.
  Evidence-policy notes for this pass. PMID:1714544, De Vivo 1991 in the New
  England Journal of Medicine, is the original clinical description but has no
  abstract in PubMed, so it cannot carry a snippet-verified evidence item and
  is acknowledged here rather than cited. PMID:20830593, an acetazolamide
  response letter, likewise has no cached content, so acetazolamide is not
  listed as a treatment; note that GeneReviews lists acetazolamide among the
  anti-seizure medications relatively contraindicated during ketogenic diet
  therapy. Orphanet ORPHA:71277 is not reachable through the structured-source
  cache, so no Orphanet frequency rows are cited. Two PMIDs supplied in the
  curation brief were wrong and were replaced after PubMed verification: Seidner
  1998 was given as 9620772, which is an EXT1 paper, and is actually
  PMID:9462754; Tang 2017 was given as 28211455, which is a physics paper, and
  is actually PMID:28106060. Five HP CURIEs offered by the Perplexity report
  were mislabelled and were not used, namely HP:0011610, HP:0007295, HP:0001942,
  HP:0002211 and HP:0001252 for paroxysmal dyskinesia, exercise-induced
  dyskinesia, hypoglycorrhachia, decreased CSF lactate and epileptic
  encephalopathy; the correct bindings used here are HP:0007166, HP:0011972 and
  HP:0030086. Caffeine avoidance, often mentioned clinically, is not in the
  GeneReviews abstract and is therefore not asserted. Evidence taken from the
  2020 international consensus statement and the 2025 narrative review is
  graded evidence_source OTHER rather than HUMAN_CLINICAL, because those
  documents report expert consensus and synthesis rather than a study cohort.
